Edge Intelligence

Deep learning-enabled edge computing

Shajulin Benedict

Chapter 7

Performance analysis of edge-enabled applications

One important step to developing an efficient edge-AI application is to diligently plan their executions based on performance. In general, performance studies are a key to improving the development of applications. The method of capturing an appropriate performance metric and planning improvement steps is considered a valid step to improve future developments. This chapter delves into the performance aspects of edge-AI applications and their potential improvement aspects.

7.1 Performance concerns

Edge-AI applications, similar to other scientific applications, are prone to performance concerns. In addition, there are a few design-specific performance concerns in edge-AI systems. In fact, application developers have to look into the capabilities of applications when executed in varying heterogeneous execution environments. In this way, they can improve the quality of edge-AI application execution and the future development of such edge-AI applications.

The performance concerns of edge-AI applications are classified into four broad categories: (a) model-specific, (b) architecture-specific, (c) algorithm-specific, and (d) data-specific. The details of these different categories are explained in the following sections.

7.2 Model-specific performance concerns

Learning models for edge-AI applications are distributed across multiple clusters. Federated environments are often considered while performing the distributed learning. In this regard, there is a potential possibility of model-related performance issues. Some of the reasons why models are prone to performance concerns are listed below:

  1. The learning models are often partitioned into smaller chunks of computation in edge-AI applications. Notably, DNN models are split into cut layers so that a few layers are executed on specified computing nodes or edge clusters. The partitioning stage in learning models can lead to failures or mis-configured situations on some occasions due to the non-availability of powerful computational nodes at a given point in time. Evidently, this situation raises performance concerns in edge-AI applications. Understanding the requirements and challenges involved in this process, the authors of [12] developed an adaptive split learning framework for learning algorithms.
  2. A few learning models are implemented as functions to trigger computational units. At times, the resources are not carefully mapped to the required configurations. The configurational mismatch could lead to performance bottlenecks.
  3. The layers of deep learning models are subject to different computational requirements. Hence, training models on homogeneous computing machines or incompatible heterogeneous machines could lead to performance concerns.

7.3 Architecture-specific performance concerns

The performance of edge-AI applications is impacted due to differing architectures (see figure 7.1). Architectures, in general, involve computing machines and the algorithms that drive computations on them. The architectures in edge-AI applications are dependent on the various factors listed below:

  1. Hardware-dependent Factors: The hardware-dependent factors relate to designing efficient caches, enabling memory access methods, utilizing appropriate compute resources, etc.
  2. Software-dependent Factors: The software-dependent factors suggest the utilization of better operating systems, distributed learning algorithms, and optimally mapped computational algorithms to underlying hardware units.
  3. Integration-related Factors: Edge-AI applications require sophisticated communication protocols and integration tools inherent to architectures. For instance, MQTT protocols are sufficient in certain edge-AI applications when compared to applying S-MQTT protocols.

7.3.1 Performance concerns: hardware-dependent

In general, computer architecture has followed uniform, non-uniform, and cache-only memory access patterns. The architecture of uniform memory access-based microcontroller or microprocessor devices is designed such that the memory controller can access the corresponding memory units uniformly. Although the access time is uniform, it has poor performance while scaling applications on these devices, i.e., a single memory controller has to deal with all connected memory units.

The advantages of the use of non-uniform memory access-based architectures are that they increase the performance of applications: (a) increasing the performance of applications in near-real-time, (b) supporting a variety of applications with the involvement of multiple memory controllers.

In some applications, we can use cache-only memory access patterns. In cache-only memory access-based architectures, the non-uniform memory access hardware is supported with caches. This means that the memory access is not only based on the location of the memory but also the state and tag associated with the memory units. In fact, the advantages of having different memory access patterns are dependent on the costs involved and space considerations. Notably, cache-only memory access architectures will be much more expensive and complex when compared to simple, uniform memory access-based architectures. The performance, in contrast, offers the opposite results in terms of costs.

The authors of [14] designed a collaborative caching solution to improve the inference time involved in edge-enabled applications. The authors focused on the data involved in the edge-based applications and their caching patterns. The notion was to involve multiple computing nodes at the edge computing level for caching the most frequently utilized data so that the performance of inferences will be faster than with traditional approaches.

Edge-AI applications often need to be ported across computing machines over time. This is because of the distributed nature of computing platforms The hardware-level implementation, therefore, has to involve virtualization techniques for quick portability. For instance, Docker technology delivers an operating system-level virtualization environment where union file systems are maintained to enable a lightweight implementation. It is obvious that Docker-level implementations offer higher portability and self-contained application-related configurations. The authors of [2] developed a lightweight virtualization environment in edge nodes to bridge the gap between cloud and edge-level sensor devices.

In many situations, Docker containers are combined with scalable frameworks such as Kubernetes. The Kubernetes framework can create multiple replicas of Docker instances that work under controlled IP addresses named PODs. The Kubernetes framework has features to orchestrate workloads that are executed on Dockers. The framework could distribute workloads across multiple compute nodes based on the underlying loads. Also, in some situations, there is a requirement for scaling compute instances, especially when faults occur in applications.

7.3.2 Performance concerns: software-dependent

There is no software that fits with all working environments. In recent years, almost all AI-based applications have been moved toward edge nodes rather than processing them in clouds. Having observed such situations, it is crucial to characterize edge-AI applications to satisfy the performance needs of the users. For instance, a video analytic-related application or an audio analytic-related application has to process the streaming data in real-time.

The applications might undergo several stages of execution, such as pre-processing, modeling, prediction, etc. In particular, multiple functions are involved in processing raw data, i.e., functions such as resizing, normalizing, transformations, etc. These functions of streaming applications have to be dealt with within a specified period of time with appropriate hardware devices, including accelerators.

Operating systems in embedded devices or microcontrollers for edge-AI applications contribute to the performance of applications. Notably, this piece of software amalgamation utilizes compute resources rather than allocating all available resources to applications. The authors of [13] compared the performance of two open-source operating systems that are popular in the embedded arena: freeRTOS and ZephyrRTOS.

In general, operating systems aim to increase the utility of resources, monitor the execution of applications, and control the execution of parallel processes on devices such as ESPs or Raspberry Pis.

With the advancement of over 30 years of research, an open-source operating system named FreeRTOS has contributed to the edge-AI domain, including AWS-based edge nodes. FreeRTOS is a suitable operating system that is often utilized in microcontrollers such as ESPs. The operating system has provisions to control or manage resources and offer services to edge-AI applications without much inconvenience to application developers or users.

Without the utilization of operating systems, the applications will not know when to execute them while mapping the available resources at a particular period of time. Additionally, these code snippets could enrich the utility and auditing capabilities of individual applications. A well-written operating system could manage I/O peripherals and sensor/actuator devices while collecting or distributing data. In summary, the software components of the operating system attempt to optimize the performance of the underlying system while running edge-AI applications.

7.3.3 Performance concerns: integration-related

The performance of edge-AI applications is impacted based on the integration of compute devices and storage units in sensor-based environments. The sensors are responsible for sensing data and inducing actuators to perform actions. Depending on the sensor data and the communication protocols, the performance of applications varies. The integration between sensors and actuators through connected devices has to be strengthened in frameworks or integration platforms in order to achieve better performance.

For instance, in industrial IoT platforms, tens of thousands of sensors and actuators are networked through various communication means, including field buses [6]. The integration environments play a key role in determining the automation processes with the utmost reliability or sensitivity. Additionally, the costs involved in the integration of devices while transferring data between edge-AI applications can significantly vary depending on the field buses, such as Modbus, Ethernet Control Automation, Process Field Networks, etc.

One of the major integration issues in any Edge-AI application is the utilization of an appropriate communication protocol. In the application stack, devices often connect to gateways or services through device-level functionalities or wiring topologies. For instance, ESP32 devices can directly connect to cloud-based services or nearby edge-level computing nodes; LoRaWAN devices can connect to gateways before sending data to cloud-assisted services. Also, the bandwidth and power limitations hinder the networking capabilities of these devices. Obviously, the integration of sensors and actuators through varying communication protocols can have a strong impact on the underlying application’s performance.

7.4 Algorithm-specific performance concerns

The performance of edge-AI applications can be improved by tuning the algorithms involved in them, apart from hardware recommendations. There are several opportunities, as listed in the following sections.

7.4.1 Compiler-specific refinements

The performance of applications can be improved if the algorithms are compiled specifically for the underlying hardware units. The advantage of involving compilers is to understand the characteristics of the underlying hardware, especially the access patterns of applications. By doing so, the application can be fine-tuned to improve its performance.

A few edge-enabled machines that are more powerful than microcontrollers, such as intel-based fanless devices, have features to select compiler flags considering the optimization features. For instance, flags such as -fO3, -fO2, etc, can induce optimization in application-specific code sequences considering the hardware features of devices, including caches [3]. In distributed environments, compilers are tuned to varying edge-level hardware units.

7.4.2 Tuning applications

Applications can be tuned considering the battery efficiency, brightness level of display devices, switching to power-safe mode, managing sleep modes, turning ON and OFF services, turning ON and OFF hardware devices, automating services, and reducing computations.

Battery efficiency is often achieved by diligently controlling the utilization of embedded devices. Also, the batteries are not charged to their maximum level to achieve a longer battery life. The batteries have to be managed effectively, considering several parameters such as voltage, current utilization, and device utilities. The batteries, typically, have different characteristics for discharging or charging electrons.

The brightness level of display units on edge devices adjusts the energy consumption of the devices. An increase in brightness levels can have a direct impact on the utility of devices. For instance, in modern devices, ambient-light sensor-based circuits are utilized to automatically vary the brightness of display devices. Accordingly, devices are able to minimize the brightness level of display devices when there are sufficient natural lights.

In general, the power-saving mode increases the lifetime of batteries. In several edge-enabled devices, the batteries are backed up with renewable energy sources. These power sources are often scarce. Obviously, the utilization of power-saving modes in devices could enhance the lifetime efficiency of applications. In some edge-AI applications, the power-saving mode is automatically enacted through APIs, considering parameters such as the availability of power sources.

Increasing the lifetime of applications can indirectly improve their performance. There are a few techniques to improve the lifetime of applications. For instance, enabling sleep modes during the idle time of applications can increase the battery lifetime of devices. The sleep modes are practiced at hierarchical levels. For instance, ESP32 switches OFF WiFi modules at the higher level of sleep mode, whereas the CPU is switched OFF during the deeper instantiating sleep modes. The authors of [9] proposed a solution that switches between ultra-low-power mode and low-power mode depending on the utility of embedded devices.

In a few modern devices, the dynamic voltage-frequency scaling (DVFS) technique is followed to adappropriate to the performance requirements of applications. By increasing the clock frequency of CPUs, the instructions are processed on a larger scale when compared to the reverse situation. Obviously, the energy consumption of applications when executed at a higher clock speed will be higher than otherwise. Other researchers have studied the DVFS government policies for embedded devices, understanding the underlying energy efficiency of the approach. [8].

Additionally, there are opportunities to perform power gating or clock gating techniques on embedded devices to increase the lifetime of applications. In the power gating approach, a few underutilized components are switched OFF; in the clock gating approach, a few underutilized components, often CPU devices, are not provided with clock signals to process the instructions or signals.

Sometimes the performance of applications can be improved by reducing the computation involved in them. For instance, if there is no need to obtain very accurate predictions, it is sufficient to reduce the number of iterations while computing learning models.

7.4.3 Selective packages

Choosing the right package for executing applications can lead to performance improvements. In general, there are several implementations for executing algebraic calculations. However, only a few implementations have mapping configurations that fit the hardware units. For instance, the MKL library for intel-based compute machines can have poor performance if the same packages are utilized in ARM or IBM power machines.

Additionally, Node.js-based implementations are sensitive to certain working environments. For instance, npm v1.3.17 does not install dependencies if the same requirements that are specified in the package.json file are satisfied. Obviously, the execution of such applications could hamper performance if they are not diligently handled. Similarly, appropriate API references and abstractions are required to handle embedded services such as WIFI, HTTP, logging, networking, etc.

7.4.4 Programming models

The programming model chosen for edge-AI applications will have a direct impact on the underlying performance of logic. Even though business logic could be developed in any programming language, very selective programming models fit well with certain situations.

Big data applications, which need to be processed using files, can be implemented using map-reduce programming models, including Hadoop. Streaming applications that involve audio or video processing can utilize Apache Spark. Also, distributed computing using hybrid programming models such as MPI and OpenMP could fit in specific use-cases. For instance, a fault-tolerant application should not consider an MPI-based programming model while developing its business logic. Obviously, choosing an appropriate programming model will have a major impact on the performance of applications.

Figure 7.2 illustrates the probable performance concerns that are caused by algorithms or application developers who code their applications indifferently.

7.5 Data-specific performance concerns

Data is considered one of the key concerns for improving the performance of edge-AI applications. In edge-AI applications, it is beneficial to have data closer to sensors or actuators. There are several reasons why data has to be closer to users, i.e., privacy and execution requirements have a direct impact while executing applications.

Some approaches in which data can be tweaked to improve the performance of edge-AI applications are listed as follows:

  1. Data distribution: If edge-AI applications have options to distribute data across multiple compute nodes, it is better to evenly distribute them across the available nodes. Researchers in the past have studied the impact of the performance of applications while transferring workloads in a dynamic fashion, considering the feedback of executions.
  2. Data grouping: It is efficient to pre-process data based on its characteristics before executing the applications. For instance, video-related applications could be pre-processed and grouped so that they could be channeled to fast-moving network channels. in contrast, UDP-related packets could be delivered on highly reliable networks to improve the performance of applications.
  3. Data partitioning: In addition to practicing different data distribution patterns, it is also better to partition data, aiming for performance improvements in computing environments. An adaptive nature of partitioning data can pave the way for conglomerating data from multiple applications, considering the limited availability of resources.
  4. Quantization: Data quantization attempts to map a series of continuous data points to a smaller set of data points. This reduces the computational time and compute resources required for creating a training model. Developing a robust data quantization technique leverages the performance efficiency of edge-AI applications.

Figure 7.3 shows the performance improvement options that could be adopted in edge-AI frameworks by improving the associated data of applications.

7.6 Performance monitoring: a need

The performance concerns of edge-AI applications have to be captured and rectified. There have been efforts in the past to capture the performance issues of enervated applications. Additionally, careful monitoring is appreciated among users owing to its potential speed up. Monitoring the performance of edge-AI applications, in general, has the advantages discussed below.

7.6.1.1 Bottleneck prediction

It is a known fact that applications or learning models execute well on powerful compute machines. However, transferring data or models across a stringent bandwidth of networks is prone to substantial bottlenecks. Similarly, executing models on low-power, computationally inefficient devices such as the ESP32 can lead to computational bottlenecks due to limited data transfer buses. In some research works, to override such bottlenecks, chip manufacturers have developed a few computing-in-memory systems so that computations are carried out nearer to the data where they reside.

Obviously, the potential bottlenecks of edge-AI applications are predicted well in advance, and the corresponding applications are tuned to improve their performance.

7.6.1.2 Resource identification

Observing poor performances or behavior of edge-AI applications notifies users and application developers about the cause of the failures or the reasons for success. One of the most challenging aspects of edge-level applications is poor resource usage. Understanding the performance of applications paves the way for selecting appropriate resources in future execution rounds.

7.6.1.3 Visualization

As many resources and compute nodes will be involved in edge-enabled AI applications, it is mandatory to visualize the performance bottlenecks and pinpoint the severity of those bottlenecks. In fact, users or application developers will be engaged in defining logics or utilizing them rather than debugging the issues, if any. Therefore, it is essential to monitor the performance of applications and pinpoint those issues easily.

7.6.1.4 Feedback

Application developers and product developers are keen to understand the user experience and offer suggestions to improve the applications. There is an urgent need to capture those suggestions or comments so that improvements can be made at the right time. Monitoring the performance of applications can directly or indirectly feed inputs to application developers based on such suggestions.

In addition to enhancing the quality of future software releases, offering feedback can reduce the production costs and utility costs associated with the applications. It is necessary to observe that positive feedback often motivates developers to create more innovative solutions within a short period of time.

7.7 Performance monitoring: metrics

Monitoring the performance of edge-AI applications is crucial for improving future application developments. The overall taxonomy representation of the metrics that are utilized to study the performance of edge-AI applications is shown in figure 7.4.

The impact of these performance metrics for assessing edge-AI applications is detailed as follows:

  1. Execution time: Execution time is a performance metric that measures the time required for executing edge-AI applications. The applications may be executed on a standalone machine or a combination of multiple compute machines. This metric is essential to evaluating the characteristics of applications based on underlying hardware units. For instance, the authors of [7] studied the efficiency of LSTM algorithms when executed on different edge accelerators, such as Jetson Nano, based on the execution time of agricultural applications.
  2. Hardware-level metrics: Applications, when executed on edge devices, are prone to hardware-related issues, including cache misses or instruction stalls. In all modern computationally capable devices, the hardware metrics could be assessed due to the presence of hardware monitoring counters such as instruction counters. The hardware-level metrics will provide low-level detail about the execution behavior of applications in real-time.
  3. Latency: Latency is the performance metric that measures the delay caused in the execution of applications. Latency could be measured due to network delays between edge devices or communication delays between compute components in a machine. The cause of latency is a serious concern for mission-critical, edge-enabled applications. However, in some cases, the applications can be tolerated because of the latency issues. A few researchers have developed a critical view about the latency achieved due to resource-constrained edge devices, i.e., the authors manifested that the latency of applications might become larger in numbers due to the utilization of several resource-constrained edge nodes [1].
  4. Throughput or job completion ratio: One of the metrics that determines the capability of the architectural components is termed throughput or the job completion ratio. This metric determines the number of applications or instances executed within a specified time frame. In an edge-AI framework, the throughput could be increased by carefully queuing applications and increasing the number of jobs.
  5. Reliability: Reliability is a performance metric that determines the consistency of executing the same application instances over a period of time. In some situations, the applications do not offer the same level of computation for several reasons, including the workload distribution of edge-AI applications across heterogeneous compute nodes.
  6. Scalability: Although the performance of applications can be improved by speeding up the computations, the engagement of applications across multiple compute units or increasing the number of application instances is a challenge in several cases. Scalability is a metric that evaluates the importance of scaling applications given a set of compute nodes or frameworks. This metric determines the capability of a framework or architecture for a given edge-AI application.
  7. Availability: The availability metric ensures the readiness of compute nodes for computing applications. In most cases, compute resources are not readily available to execute edge-AI applications. This minimizes the performance of applications.
  8. Performance–power–space optimization: There is a trade-off between the performance, power, and space requirements of applications. Increasing the performance of applications can speed up their execution. However, the power consumption and utilization of components could significantly increase for the application. Obviously, there is a trade-off between the performance and energy consumption of applications. Also, in order to increase the performance of applications, there is a tendency to compute applications utilizing multiple compute resources in parallel. This feature can increase the space requirements or memory capacity of executing applications. Hence, in many use-cases, researchers attempt to optimize performance–power–space metrics while executing applications on specified compute nodes.

7.8 Energy-efficiency methods

Energy is a serious concern for battery-operated embedded devices that are utilized in edge-AI applications. This section explores the energy efficiency aspects of edge computing and the research directions in this domain.

In general, power consumption and energy consumption vary based on the application of improvement techniques in the edge-AI domain. Typically, power is considered the multiplication of voltage (V) and current (I). Power consumption relates to the efficiency of the systems involved in executing applications. In contrast, energy is defined as the work performed in a specified time interval. The energy metric directly relates to the time involved in executing an application.

7.8.1 Energy measurements

Measuring the energy consumption of edge-AI applications is one of the most important steps in improving their efficiency. The approach to measuring energy consumption in applications differs from machine to machine. For instance, a computationally powerful edge-level machine that is utilized for executing edge-AI applications could monitor energy consumption using robust modeling techniques. But the energy measurements in a microcontroller-based edge node require instrumenting devices. Accordingly, the energy measurements at edge nodes are classified as discussed below.

7.8.1.1 Energy measurements: data center nodes

The energy consumption of edge devices that are mounted nearer to data center nodes is monitored using intelligent power distribution units. The intelligent power distribution units display the power consumed over a specified period of time. This value is correlated with the execution of edge-AI applications to monitor the energy consumption of applications.

7.8.1.2 Energy measurements: standalone nodes

Several standalone nodes, such as personal computers, laptops, and computationally powerful servers residing nearer to data, are utilized as edge nodes. These devices have in-built sensors to monitor the energy consumption of applications. For instance, Intel-based processors have built-in sensors that model the energy consumption of devices using the running average power limit (RAPL). The energy consumption values that are denoted in joules could be captured for a specified time interval when the applications are executed. There are a few implementations carried out by researchers, such as the PAPI library, to collect the energy consumption of applications using RAPL.

7.8.1.3 Energy measurements: microcontroller-based nodes

Measuring the energy consumption of applications that are executed on microcontroller-based devices is different from other devices. The reasons include the non-availability of hardware counters similar to computationally powerful devices and the non-availability of parallel threads to measure the energy consumption of applications. In addition, any continuous energy monitoring approach could become a huge overhead for such microcontrollers.

To overcome the limitations, the energy consumption of microcontrollers is measured or modeled [10] using external devices that are equipped with current measuring sensors, such as INA219 sensors. This means that a separate measurement device based on another microcontroller device may be required to monitor the energy consumption of applications that are being executed on microcontrollers. These dedicated energy measurement units measure energy consumption using external timestamps that are triggered based on the requirement of a monitoring unit.

7.8.2 Energy efficiency methods

Energy consumption in edge-AI applications is minimized in various ways. Notably, methods such as providing awareness, offering inherent control steps, utilizing appropriate energy sources, and accomplishing algorithm-specific energy efficiency techniques were popularly practiced in the past.

7.8.2.1 Energy awareness

Providing awareness about the energy consumption of edge-AI applications is one step to reducing anticipatory energy consumption. For instance, if an application developer knows that the energy consumption or battery utilization of a specified application is x joules, the developer could attempt to identify the reasons for the energy consumption. Accordingly, energy consumption could be minimized in future developments or implementations.

To do so, the energy consumption of edge-AI applications need to be monitored either at runtime or offline. If the energy consumption of edge-AI application is reported at runtime, the user could either stop the application or modify the runtime configurations to efficiently utilize the application.

7.8.2.2 Inherent control

The energy consumption of applications could be improved if a few control measures are followed. Notably, controlling CPU clock cycles, controlling compiler flags, controlling components, etc, are inherent approaches that improve the energy efficiency of edge-AI applications. The details of the control mechanisms that could be adopted in edge-based devices are listed below:

  • Controlling CPU clocks: The clock frequencies are fine-tuned based on the requirements of applications inherently. For instance, edge devices that are computationally capable have adopted dynamic voltage-frequency scaling. With the advent of DVFS, the clocks could be modified to function at varying speeds. Hence, an edge-AI application could be load-balanced, assuming the implications of CPU cycles. The feature of DVFS is inherent in many modern edge-level processors, including Jetson Nano devices.
  • Controlling compiler flags: The compiler flags could be tuned to minimize the energy consumption of applications. Flags such as -ffloat-store, -fshort-enums, and -fO3 can significantly modify the code optimizations in edge-enabled learning algorithms. The compiler flags are considered an important optimization tool to deliver energy-efficient applications.
  • Controlling components: Switching ON/OFF devices or components in edge-level nodes is a powerful method to minimize the energy consumption of applications. Arduino has features to switch ON/OFF devices, such as analog-to-digital converters, when they are not utilized, using narcoleptic libraries. Also, some software components of applications could be controlled while executing the end-to-end application. For instance, services that are required for monitoring the energy consumption of applications could be stopped if there is no requirement for it to be reported. Similarly, a few security-oriented services, such as identity mapping components, are not required in trusted working environments. Obviously, there are several opportunities to minimize the energy consumption of edge-AI applications by controlling the software and hardware components involved in their execution.
7.8.2.3 Utilizing energy sources

It is an efficient approach to utilize renewable energy sources instead of power grid sources if they are available for power-ON applications. In an edge-AI environment, edge devices could be located in several geographical locations. A few devices may be powered by renewable sources, and the remaining devices may be powered by normal power sources. A framework that is responsible for allocating edge-AI tasks to edge nodes should be diligently allocating tasks to devices that are sourced using renewable energy sources such as solar panels, wind energy sources, or hydropower sources. The advantage of having renewable energy sources is that they minimize the external carbon footprints that are possible using normal power sources, such as thermal power plants.

7.8.2.4 Algorithm-specific energy-efficiency

Edge-AI applications can be tuned to improve their energy efficiency. Carefully developing algorithms that are suitable to be executed in resource-constrained environments is one of the key steps to improving the energy efficiency of applications. In the recent past, researchers have been following several approaches at the algorithmic level to improve their energy efficiency. Some of the solutions are listed below:

  1. Datatype selection: The datatype assignment while writing an edge-AI application is diligently carried out considering the requirement of double precision or long integers. There is a tendency for any application developer to initialize a huge stack variable, although the memory requirement might not be the case. In doing so, the memory space and the hardware utility increase without real necessity. This could be easily avoided to improve the energy consumption of such applications. In fact, this method is quite crucial in microcontroller-based edge nodes where memory limitations need to be considered.
  2. Thread pinning: Computed resources need to be mapped to the data sources and the availability of data nearer to them. Thread affinity is an approach that ensures the locality of data while executing applications on parallel systems [5]. Algorithms could be designed considering the data affinity factors of the underlying hardware units. Thus, the data need not be transferred across distant devices while performing logic.
  3. Handling wait times: In general, operating systems have software modules to control wait times for instructions. The software modules could be interlinked with the application’s logic so that the wait time of applications could be minimized in edge-AI applications. In fact, there is a need to constantly assess the wait time of tasks and deliver them to available resources. At times, it is also required to increase the wait times of parallel tasks so that the clock frequencies can be modified to improve the energy efficiency of applications. For example, a parallel task executed on a faster machine ends up with a huge wait time if there are multiple instances of I/O calls. Instead, the clock frequency could be reduced during the wait times so that the energy consumption of the entire application would be minimized.

A holistic picture of the above-mentioned energy efficiency methods in edge-AI systems is given in figure 7.5.

7.9 Carbon efficiency methods

Most researchers are interested in zeroing in on carbon emissions. In fact, IoT-enabled edge-AI applications are prone to emitting a huge volume of carbon due to cooling compute nodes on cloud data centers or on edge-level accelerators. In this regard, tens of thousands of researchers have shown interest in neutralizing carbon emissions [11]. They have attempted to educate the global market about the footprints of carbon emissions. In addition, industries are being trained to prevent carbon emissions by levying policies that are more powerful than in previous decades.

In the past, researchers have worked to remove carbon emissions from industrial waste. To do so, a few researchers have developed real-time working models that showcase the number of carbon emissions from different sources around the globe. The AI-assisted models can pinpoint the forecast volume of carbon emissions while executing edge-AI applications. Accordingly, users could decide to either stop executing such applications or support some policies to override carbon emissions.

Carbon emissions will have a direct impact on the performance of applications. To increase the performance of applications, the compute machines are powered ON and processed at a high speed. This attempt can directly heat up the machines and induce cooling systems to indirectly impact carbon emissions in our environment. In a large running edge-AI application, it is sufficient to remove certain execution instances so that carbon emissions can be minimized.

There have been efforts in the past to employ hierarchical computing architectures so that a tree of computing infrastructure could be removed from computations in an edge-AI application. In doing so, carbon emissions could be minimized. Additionally, adopting load-balancing features or data locality concepts could incrementally minimize the carbon emissions of applications.

In some countries, carbon emission policies are finalized so that penalties are framed for defaulters. In order to avoid data immutability, blockchain ledgers are utilized to record the volume of carbon emissions, especially in corrupt industrial locations. As part of carbon emission neutrality, some organizations have attempted to plant trees to neutralize the amount of carbon emissions from their plants. Obviously, data centers that are responsible for emitting carbon emissions will have to plant an equal volume of trees in the name of reforestation to compensate for the carbon emissions. A comprehensive survey of the carbon neutrality procedures adopted by various researchers in the past is discussed in [4].

7.10 Workload scheduling and performance impacts

Scheduling tasks and routing them to different compute nodes in edge-enabled applications have a direct impact on performance. In general, scheduling is a process that allocates resources among the allocated compute nodes. On an edge-cloud platform, this decision is quite cumbersome owing to several factors, such as maximizing resource utilization, minimizing energy consumption, minimizing carbon emissions, minimizing latency, etc.

In edge-AI applications, the most important task is to offload learning models across geographically distributed compute nodes, surpassing from edge to cloud. This is a serious concern for scheduling algorithm designers or policymakers. Due to the ad hoc nature involved in the situation, allocating tasks to the available pool of compute resources is a challenging task.

In the past, researchers have suggested ideas to develop scheduling algorithms, mostly heuristic ones, considering the scheduling objectives. Notably, genetic algorithms, particle swarm optimization, ant volony optimization, and honey bee optimization algorithms have been practiced by a few researchers while deploying workloads across multiple compute nodes of the edge-cloud computing continuum. Apart from the regular meta-heuristic algorithms, there have been efforts to study the impact of the adaptive nature or dynamism involved in the scheduling platforms A few researchers have compared the available scheduling algorithms with the traditional FCFS, or greedy algorithms.

The scheduling decisions are dependent on algorithms that decide the positioning of edge-AI tasks on compute nodes. The scheduling algorithms could establish a schedule such that the tasks are deployed to compute nodes that are powered by renewable energy sources. Accordingly, carbon emissions are minimized in an edge-AI framework. Sufficient research is carried out to look into the best possible scheduling algorithm that can be utilized in any working environment. For instance, a few algorithms are so computationally intensive that they cannot be executed on computationally constrained devices, including ESPs.

Other researchers have explored online scheduling approaches. The online scheduling approaches are highly dynamic in nature. This enables timely decisions considering the dynamism involved in the edge-cloud compendium.

In the existing literature, there are a few limitations in the scheduling algorithms, i.e., the scheduling algorithms have not considered the hidden costs involved in network bandwidth or VM instances utilized for executing the algorithms. In the future, researchers could investigate adaptability or communication-related issues while integrating scheduling algorithms into edge-AI frameworks.

7.11 Performance monitoring tools

Monitoring the performance of applications is an essential step to improving the performance of edge-AI applications. It is essential to understand the performance issues of edge-AI applications and report the issues to the user. This is a mandatory step for application developers to improve the development of edge-AI applications.

In modern situations, monitoring applications are enacted using performance monitoring tools. The tools are necessary due to the following use-cases:

  1. It is necessary to showcase the performance problems in a user-friendly manner.
  2. The tools are software components that scale automatically along with the applications. For instance, if an edge-AI application is implemented from edge to cloud, the monitoring tool must scale along with the computations of the devices.
  3. The performance monitoring tool has to have a limited overhead when compared with applications. This means that the tool itself should not become a burden for the user by executing monitoring-related functions.

In principle, performance monitoring of edge-AI applications could be adopted in five broad categories: the tracing approach, the sandboxing approach, the modeling approach, the simulation approach, and the online approach. The crucial features of these monitoring approaches and their insights are given as follows.

7.11.1 Tracing approach

In the tracing approach, the performance of edge-AI applications is measured after collecting all relevant performance metrics, such as execution time of functions, energy consumption of code snippets, etc. In this approach, the application has to be completed before sniffing over the code portions and the relevant performance monitoring results. The tracing approach is simple due to the measurement pattern that traverses along with the application. In many cases, the approach is also termed an offline monitoring approach.

The major advantage of measuring the performance of applications using a tracing approach is the capability of producing customizable performance analysis reports. The visualization of the performance analysis can be enhanced due to the availability of a large volume of performance data. Additionally, the approach is naive, as there is no need to evaluate the performance of applications in real-time.

However, the major drawback of this approach is the collection of a large volume of performance analysis data. Obviously, a separate processing time is required to study the performance concerns of applications.

7.11.2 Sandboxing approach

Similar to the sandboxes created in playgrounds to ensure a safe playing environment for children, the sandboxing approach in computing systems enables a separate environment to study the performance of edge-AI applications. The approach expects the devices to be compatible with a real-world working environment. The sandboxing environment is an isolated environment that mimics the edge-AI working environment. Hence, users or application developers can learn the probable performance impacts of applications while changing certain runtime parameters, such as the number of compute instances or CPU cycles.

In general, the approach expects a tedious implementation considering various factors of the edge-AI application and the underlying hardware setup. Obviously, the skillset required to develop such a performance analysis environment is difficult in many use-cases. However, once the environment is set up, the sandboxes could provide a more realistic performance detection platform for observing the working behavior of applications.

7.11.3 Modeling approach

The modeling approach expects the performance analysis tool developer to create mathematical models that mimic the edge-AI framework. The modeling approach works based on the prior history and the identification of variables based on the historical data. The historical data suggests variations in the performance of applications due to hardware and allied factors. Accordingly, equations could be modeled so that future experiments are evaluated based on them.

In the modeling approach, the performance of applications is studied after the data is classified into three broad categories, such as training data, validation data, and testing data. The training data consists of performance measures of edge-AI applications that relate to factors, including hardware metrics. Based on the training data, mathematical models are created for assessing the performance of applications. Later, the generated mathematical models are evaluated based on the validation data. During this phase, the mathematical model that mimics the performance model is fine-tuned to attain the utmost clarity. Finally, the testing phase expects that the performance of applications can be evaluated without any further implementations.

The major advantages of this performance analysis approach are the evaluation of the performance of applications without executing them. This is possible based on the mathematical model created. However, the approach expects a large volume of previous historical data to establish an accurate performance prediction model.

7.11.4 Simulation approach

In the simulation approach, the performance analysis of edge-AI applications is derived using simulation patterns. The simulation approach mimics a real-world situation where the developer will have a strong knowledge of the environment. In this approach, users can easily modify parameters to study the performance behavior of edge-AI applications. They can scale certain parameters, such as the number of nodes or communication protocols, without executing applications. As mentioned, developing a simulator is a challenging task due to the requirement of a thorough skillset in hardware and software scenarios.

7.11.5 Online approach

Developing a tool that monitors the performance of applications in real-time is a very challenging task. This is because the overhead of a performance monitoring tool can increase if it is not developed, considering all situations. Typically, the applications are instrumented using specific functions that are invoked at runtime to monitor their performances. For instance, a few functions are developed to mention the start and stop of measurements in applications. These functions establish a stack for measuring appropriate performance metrics such as hardware counters, execution time, etc.

The drawback of this approach is the probable overhead that is anticipated during the execution of applications. in contrast, the tracing approach had a large number of overheads without impacting the regular execution of applications.

As can be observed, the online-based performance monitoring approach is advantageous for making early decisions. For instance, if an edge-AI application has a potential performance flaw, the entire application could be stopped at the initial stage of execution as the performance values will be visible to the user.

Figure 7.6 depicts the most commonly utilized performance monitoring approaches. The approaches are detailed in previous subsections.

7.12 Cloud/fog/edge-level performance monitoring

Performance analysis tools are available as standalone devices that operate on clouds, fogs, or edge nodes. However, research efforts are in progress to develop an integrated performance analysis tool for an entire edge-AI application.

In the present situation, researchers apply available standalone performance analysis tools to monitor the performance of applications. For instance, tools such as AWS Xray, AWS cloud watch, Google Stackdriver, AppDynamics, Wavefront, Epsagon, Thundra, and Application Insights have manifested the performance monitoring of cloud-based applications. These tools are often utilized in computationally intensive clusters or fog nodes.

Additionally, tools such as Prometheus, InfluxDB, and Grafana have been utilized by several open-source enthusiasts in container-based working environments. However, there are very few performance analysis tools that are dedicated to the edge-AI computing continuum. The major drawback of designing such a performance analysis tool is that it scales the monitoring feature across multiple heterogeneous computing environments along with the applications. Not only that, the application has to perform the monitoring tasks without much overhead in real-time. Hence, the development of an end-to-end performance monitoring tool for edge-AI applications is still a research challenge.

7.13 Conclusion

Analyzing the performance of edge-AI applications can improve their applicability. Developers may need to focus on developing application logic rather than studying the impact of performance issues when executed on edge-enabled devices. This chapter studies the importance of performance analysis in edge-based frameworks. Additionally, it reveals the potential energy consumption issues of embedded devices and the techniques to monitor the associated issues. The chapter provides a taxonomy of performance monitoring in edge-AI domains.

Edge Intelligence
