Part IV

Applications

IOP Publishing

Edge Intelligence

Deep learning-enabled edge computing

Shajulin Benedict

Chapter 11

Edge-AI applications

Edge-enabled AI applications have been widely applied in various domains such as industry, healthcare, education, forensics, robotics, etc. This chapter explores the importance of edge-level AI for such application domains.

11.1 Applications

Applications are software components that are designed to assist users or other software modules. The applications serve certain user-defined objectives that mimic the requirements of real-world use cases. The application of edge-level intelligence in combination with cloud systems has increasingly evolved in recent years owing to technologies such as IoT and ML/DL. There are several domains in which edge intelligence could be applied: (a) healthcare, (b) manufacturing, (c) industries, (d) agriculture, (e) forensics, (f) logistics, and (g) robotics. The following sections will dive into the potential applications and probable use-cases that require edge-level intelligence for applications.

11.2 Edge-AI for healthcare

Healthcare applications are significantly increasing day by day because people wish to live longer. In fact, there are several innovations to protect ourselves from potential healthcare risks if appropriate data analytics is followed. For instance, a patient could proactively take medicines if the health-related data pinpoints near-future health hazards using AI technologies. This section explores the potential use cases of applying edge-level intelligence to the healthcare sector.

11.2.1 Remote healthcare monitoring and prediction

Remote healthcare monitoring and prediction have enormous applications in the COVID era. The outbreak of the COVID pandemic resulted in many chronic patients staying at home and not visiting doctors for many months, who were suffering from asthma, diabetics, cardiac problems, etc. This increased the application of remote healthcare monitoring using sophisticated technologies, including IoT.

11.2.1.1 Remote monitoring

Remote healthcare monitoring is a healthcare application that involves patients, doctors, hospitals, and organizations to provide remote healthcare consultation and monitoring [3]. The healthcare monitoring system includes devices such as mobile phones, sensor-enabled devices, personal gadgets, and some hospital equipment to provide sophisticated health-related checkups, monitoring, or predictions to remote patients.

For instance, mobile phones could be used to get appropriate remote healthcare consultations or diet advice from connected hospitals or doctors. In some cases, remote healthcare monitoring is useful to remind patients to correctly take their medicines. It is always possible that chronic patients either forget to take tablets or take their medicines multiple times, forgetting that they had already taken their medicines.

Figure 11.1 illustrates the architecture diagram for remote healthcare monitoring applications where edge-level computing of in-body sensors or on-body sensors is carried out. The architecture analyzes data with the assistance of remotely connected hospitals.

11.2.1.2 Prediction

Apart from remotely monitoring and notifying the health status of remote patients, it is also beneficial to forecast the future impact of the health conditions based on the monitoring data. For instance, if a patient is constantly registering blood pressure levels over 140, the remote healthcare system could forecast the possible health issues. It could also advise the patients on appropriate changes to their diet.

Prediction of health conditions could be obtained using prediction algorithms such as Support Vector Machines, Random Forests, Regressions, etc. A few deep learning algorithms could be applied to study the nightime sleep issues of patients. Notably, a diabetic patient could be injected with the right volume of glucose during the night by monitoring the sugar level and predicting sleep activity using Deep Learning algorithms.

Engaging edge-AI in medical data, including images, increases the chances of detecting health-related issues. For instance, CNN-based algorithms could be utilized to detect the types of skin-related cancers. The edge devices can internally process images and identify the type of skin cancer based on several parameters, such as skin color, infected region size, spreading nature of the skin, etc. Apart from being a specialized tool for early detection of health issues, it can become a prominent indicator for doctors while performing remote monitoring or diagnosis of patients.

11.2.1.3 An architecture

The remote healthcare monitoring system requires edge-level computations, as personal data should be protected by public parties. Although the majority of the services are based on the cloud, the data has to be prevented from being exposed to public repositories. Hence, the architecture that deals with the remote healthcare monitoring ideas has to be highly secure.

The remote healthcare monitoring system should include the following entities:

  1. User-interface: A robust user interface that has 24/7 software bots to automatically share appropriate preliminary information and that suggests responses in a user-friendly manner is required in the healthcare monitoring system. The user interface supports virtual reality-based solutions that could be hosted not only on cloud systems but also on edge-enabled patient sites.
  2. Sensors or end devices: Remote healthcare monitoring includes multiple types of sensors and edge-level compute nodes. Sensors such as temperature, ECG, heart rate sensor, etc, have to be periodically monitored depending on the nature of the applications. The sensor data needs to be pre-processed using edge-level computing nodes so that the data is not transferred to third-party compute nodes.Healthcare monitoring devices are categorized as in-body devices, on-body devices, mobile devices, ambulatory devices, and hospital devices. The purpose of in-body devices is to automatically capture or activate certain sensory data. For instance, sensor-enabled devices that are placed inside the body to continually report health conditions are termed in-body devices. The in-body sensors are often required by chronic patients, e.g., glucometers can be placed inside the body to automatically monitor the blood sugar levels of patients; the on-body devices are sensor-enabled devices that serve as wearable devices, consumables, fashion galleries, and assistive devices. The on-body devices can help not only chronic patients but also users who like to check the status of their health at a specified time interval. Mobile devices are sensor-enabled medical devices that are not fixed to a geographical space. Ambulatory devices are IoT-enabled sensor/actuator devices that are fixed in ambulances to treat remote patients who need special attention prior to visiting hospitals. Hospital devices are devices that are available in hospitals. These devices are numbered and located based on geo-stationary information.
  3. AI components: The data collected from various sensor-enabled devices in a remote healthcare monitoring system includes AI-based algorithms. These AI algorithms are utilized to either predict or classify data based on features identified in datasets.
11.2.1.4 Priority monitoring

Based on the health monitoring conditions and the availability of resources, the remote health monitoring system has to prioritize tasks. For instance, a periodic remote monitoring service should not require many resources for a normal user. Instead, it has to deliver resources to a critically ill patient. This reveals that the remote monitoring tasks need to be queued up for certain tasks.

Accordingly, the tasks involved in the remote monitoring system are classified as: (i) critical tasks, i.e. tasks that need to have an immediate attention, (ii) periodic tasks, the remote health monitoring tasks that have to be constantly activated over time, (iii) preventive tasks, tasks that are expected to detect cancer early or potential health risks using sophisticated AI algorithms, (iv) federated tasks, tasks that need to be collaboratively handled by multiple compute nodes or organizations, (v) alarming tasks: the healthcare-related tasks that have to provide warning messages or notifications, and (vi) educational tasks which are the tasks that provide some insights about health risks if some habits are not followed on time. Hence, priority-based monitoring of tasks in a remote healthcare monitoring system enable actuating tasks depending on the priority or importance of tasks and the availability of compute resources.

11.2.2 Personalized medicine

Personalized medicine can offer more accurate medication tailored to the need for medicines. The objective of having personalized medicine is to increase the accuracy of treatments and improve the well-being of patients. There are cases where doctors prescribe medicines that are generic for diseases. This happens due to the medical treatment provided by less experienced doctors. Specialist doctors are then overloaded due to having to deliver personalized medicine to patients due to a large number of patients in hospitals.

With the remote monitoring solutions, patients could get more personalized attention as they will be assisted by IoT-enabled sensor nodes. Patients with high glucose content will be prescribed non-glucose-based medicines even if they have a high fever or other medical issues. Edge-AI solutions play a crucial role in such personalized medicine as sensor data is kept close to the end users.

11.2.3 Stress release systems

In the modern world, many people work as machines, while machines have started to work smartly. The stress level of people working in a number of organizations, including academic organizations, remains high due to the committed completion of daily tasks. It is crucial to identify the stress levels of such employees so that employers do not lose these employees and the families of the employees do not suffer hardship.

Edge-AI solutions can solve stress-related issues if the behavior of employees is captured using video-connected systems. The root cause of the increasing stress levels could be sorted out based on the sensed data and inputs from various data points. In this way, the intelligence is applied at the edge, where users are located, without sending the data to cloud services.

The emotions of humans, such as anger, sadness, happiness, disgust, etc, can be easily detected using edge-enabled machines [14]. These devices parse video frames and capture emotions using computationally-intensive deep learning algorithms such as YOLO, CNN, ResNET, etc. Detecting the emotions of school children can pave the way to directly dealing with anomalies and health issues quickly.

11.3 Industrial applications using edge-AI

Edge AI is widely utilized in industry. In general, industries marked several revolutions, such as Industry 1.0, Industry 2.0, Industry 3.0, and Industry 4.0. The processes involved in production environments are enriched with newer techniques and methods, such that the revenue of the industry has remarkably increased. Figure 11.2 provides a high-level overview of the list of industrial applications that benefit from using edge-AI. These applications will be discussed in the following section.

In Industry 1.0, the products were developed without any specific process improvement techniques. Product development was slow in the 18th century. During this period, steam engines were used to significantly increase production. With the advent of Industry 2.0, assembly-level production started using electricity. The discovery of electricity in the early 19th century has allowed a massive increase in products over time. However, the assembly production unit has to be carefully managed by a group of managers and full-time workers. Industry 3.0 improved the communication medium between organizations and managers who were involved in production using the internet. The use of internet technologies minimized the time it took to deliver products. For instance, the use of postal communication to order products and subcomponents delayed the production processes. However, email-based communications reduced the time required for end-to-end product delivery to the market.

Industry 4.0 has ushered in a remarkable shift in production processes industry. It has applied IoT-based sensor technologies to automatically detect the requirements of raw materials and decide on the number of products to be delivered on time. The entire process of product development was able to happen without human or semi-human interventions. This led to the application of edge-AI techniques by learning the production requirements and customer requirements in a mass production environment. The edge-AI techniques enhanced the productivity and revenues of industries by modifying the processes involved in the traditional manufacturing system.

11.3.1 Predictive maintenance

Predictive maintenance is an approach where maintenance is supported by IoT-enabled technologies. In earlier cases, maintenance of equipment or machines had to be carried out by engineers after they were worn out or failed. This approach leads to poor productivity in industries, as engineers have to work around failures until maintenance is successful.

In predictive maintenance, sensors will be mounted on machines or factory units that are not frequently reachable by humans. The sensors will collect the details and working conditions of machines periodically. This data is processed to detect machine failures. For instance, the hissing noise of machines may not be noticed by a machine operator who is accustomed to being near to the machine for many years. In contrast, sensors could easily detect such minor variations in the sound produced by machines. Obviously, based on the data and the analytics of the data, maintenance could be carried out in time.

The major advantages of predictive maintenance are low costs, decreased downtime of machines, increased productivity, reduced redundant failures, improved safety, etc. Due to timely maintenance, the lifetime of machines will increase.

Traditional machine learning algorithms such as linear regression, logistic regression, random forests, support vector machines, neural networks, and other commonly utilized algorithms are used in predictive maintenance problems. Owing to structured data input from sensors connected to machines, the prediction algorithms are much faster than a few complicated deep learning algorithms. However, there are use cases to utilize deep learning-based classification algorithms to learn the maintenance-related issues of machines. For instance, ResNet-based learning algorithms could be applied to 3D printing machines to detect nozzle problems or bed leveling issues [17] from the printed outputs of machines.

11.3.2 Digital twin

A digital twin is a visual representation of a real environment’s process or product. The term was coined by Michael Grieves in 2002. NASA applied this concept to explore space objects by visually representing them. The idea of the digital twin is to connect both real and virtual objects so that a single virtual instance could manipulate millions of real objects.

The digital twin has multiple distributed sensors to collect real-time data on real objects and process it using virtual objects. For instance, a car manufacturing plant could utilize digital twin virtual objects to mass produce and alter the configurations of tens of hundreds of real manufacturing plants at one time. In this way, the manufacturing processes are productively enhanced using digital twins.

The major components of digital twins are the linking data and actuating connections [15]. The data has to be collected from multiple sensors that are connected to real-world objects. The data could be either centrally processed in the cloud or processed in a decentralized manner using edge-enabled devices. These sorts of predictions or learning inferences require a strong relationship to edge intelligence that should be processed on edge nodes.

The digital twins are deployed using cloud services in the initial stage of development. However, the evolution of edge-based computing systems has increased the application of digital twins in various domains. For instance, manufacturing and controlling car production are remotely controlled using digital twins.

Edge devices are mounted in digital twins to increase the speed involved in the manipulation of physical objects. Otherwise, the data has to be transported to clouds, and decisions have to be taken with a delay in operations. The edge-AI-supported solutions increased the security capabilities of systems. This is due to the fact that the data is not transported to third-party cloud systems. Similarly, the past operations of physical objects or machines could be tracked using edge clusters while delivering intelligence.

11.3.3 Connected factories

Connected factories are cloud-assisted manufacturing platforms that collaboratively share the resources required to produce products. The idea is to provide IoT-enabled computing solutions that could rapidly provision resources from a shared pool of configurable resources via the internet. The connected factories integrate high-end technologies into traditional manufacturing paradigms.

The major advantages of offering edge AI in connected factories include:

  1. Increased business: The business operations increase on a distributed cloud-based manufacturing platform. It is because of the shared distribution of raw materials and sub-products that a product is developed. As manufacturing and delivery units are highly distributed, the awareness of the product is high, creating a greater value for the product.
  2. Fast production: In cloud-assisted connected factories, manufacturing processes need not wait for hours or days owing to the availability of required resources. Even if the resources are not immediately available, the product manufacturing request could be taken to different geographical locations where resources are readily available.
  3. Minimal wastage: There is no need to transport raw materials to a centralized manufacturing plant in connected factories. Due to this approach, the need to order a large number of products, devices, or subcomponents can be avoided. Obviously, wastage due to the development of products is significantly minimized in connected factories.
  4. Reduced cost: There are several reasons that reduce the costs involved in manufacturing a collaborative product. Quick production, minimal waste, increased awareness, larger innovations, etc, can directly or indirectly reduce the costs involved in the manufacturing processes.
  5. Increased flexibility: It is not necessary that the entire process be centralized in shared factories. The factories and the administration are provided with the utmost autonomy to deliver their sub-products, subject to increasing the objectives. In this regard, the platform offers a huge amount of flexibility in developing collaborative products.
  6. Optimization opportunities: The connected factories induce opportunities to optimize objectives. In fact, there are potential trade-offs to designing one holistic solution. For instance, minimizing the costs involved in choosing a factory that has cheaper raw materials could lead to huge transportation costs. Hence, specialized optimization algorithms need to be formulated considering multi-objectives in connected factories.
  7. Multi-site production: Another opportunity for promoting connected factories is the possibility of involving multiple production plants from different geographical locations. For instance, a pot could be manufactured in a plant that has sufficient clay nearer to its locations, whereas painting could be carried out in a hot and dry place where natural support is available.

Figure 11.3 showcases the list of advantages that can improve business in connected factories due to the application of Edge-AI.

The purpose of involving edge-AI solutions is to provide predictive maintenance and manufacturing assistance to products [13]. Edge-assisted AI solutions can boost businesses or product delivery situations in such connected factories. Additionally, the feedback of customers could be directly input into decentralized systems, considering the local regions, if edge-AI solutions are developed for connected factories.

11.3.4 Smart tools

Manufacturing industries have marked several AI-assisted solutions apart from Digital Twins and predictive maintenance solutions. The tools involving software and hardware combine edge-level AI mechanisms to improve efficiency. A few of the most notable AI-assisted tools and solutions that are vividly utilized in the manufacturing sector are listed as follows:

  1. Error detection tools: AI-assisted edge-level solutions have become one of the crucial solutions for detecting errors in machines or industrial processes. For instance, dust intensity mounted on machines could be missed by human eyes. However, edge-AI-based cameras easily detect it and notify users.
  2. Automated tools: Several manufacturing units have employed robots or software bots to automatically reason about the functioning of machines. In certain situations, the machines may require further instructions or human intervention. These interactions are avoided by bots. For instance, ordering raw materials, collecting materials from other departments, proactively deciding on the number of required components for production, etc, could be handled by these bots automatically.
  3. Night workers: Machinery tools are designed with edge-AI mechanisms to deliver results during the night. For instance, cleaning machines, cleaning workspaces, ordering stationary items or consumables, etc, are carried out by specialized robotic tools during the night. In doing so, the requirement for lights in manufacturing plants can be avoided. Also, the specified tasks, such as cleaning operations, can be undertaken without disturbing existing useful work during the production time of the manufacturing units.

11.3.5 Optimized productivity

Edge-AI solutions are often applied to increase the productivity of industries. They are utilized in two broad approaches: (i) supply chain optimization and (ii) demand prediction. This subsection details these two approaches to increasing the productivity of industries.

11.3.5.1 Supply chain optimization

In general, large-scale manufacturing units will receive tens of thousands of orders from various parts of the globe. With the ongoing use of cloud manufacturing techniques, orders have dramatically increased for a few potential quality manufacturers. Obviously, it becomes difficult to issue commands and deliver duties to subordinates or connected machines manually. Hence, edge-assisted AI solutions will become useful in these situations.

Handling a big-data system in an automatic manner is not a difficult task for any AI-based system. For instance, a large scale computer production unit can keep track of assembled components with appropriate timestamps and delivery maps. In doing so, the AI-assisted software could easily figure out customers who purchased such computers. This approach will be beneficial for replacing the components of computers once they are sold to customers.

The edge-enabled AI systems could be combined with other technologies, such as blockchains. This combination can provide a direct sale deal with customers or a purchase deal with producers. For instance, AI-assisted blockchains can enable the direct purchase of rice from farmers without any dealers or brokers.

Proactive decisions on supply chain management using AI-based assistance can minimize the time to delivery. It can perfectly deliver goods on time, as specified in the pre-assigned rules. This is because the data relating to supply chain operations is assessed and proactively predicted to understand potential hurdles or bottlenecks in operations. The proactiveness of decisions could vary depending on the AI algorithms that are performed in supply chain operations. For instance, federated learning-based edge intelligence can boost the performance involved in supply chain operations due to the collaborative involvement of regional data to make decisions.

11.3.5.2 Demand prediction

The second factor that improves productivity in the manufacturing sector using edge-AI solutions is demand prediction. Many manufacturing plants are unaware of the exact demand for products. For instance, car manufacturers are not aware of potential demand for a particular model in a country due to the requirement of learning a large volume of data. Details such as interest, budget, country inflation rate, previous purchase data, behavior of car owners, etc, could impact the sale of cars.

Edge-AI systems can let producers understand the buying patterns for particular products. This can have direct and indirect impacts on the economy of a country. For instance, the Gross Domestic Product (GDP) rate of countries increases and the inflation rate of countries decreases if the buyer pattern and the usage pattern of products are predicted using AI-assisted systems. Notably, the sale of seasonal products such as fire crackers or Santa Claus outfits increases during November and December every year. This buyer pattern could be predicted using AI-based algorithms that are customized to regional datasets using edge-AI algorithms. North India hardly celebrates Christmas, whereas south India celebrates it.

The authors of [18] designed a demand prediction strategy for manufacturing plants by collecting the real-time demand details of the plant.

11.3.6 Interactive production

Virtual reality-assisted solutions involve edge-AI algorithms for creating interactive production environments in the manufacturing sector. The virtual reality technology contributes to developing manufacturing prototypes in the form of simulations. As the name indicates, virtual reality forms a virtual illusion of the manufacturing platform. If a manufacturer wants to experiment with the modification of the dashboard in a car, virtual reality provides a platform to view it.

Prototyping manufacturing-related products or sub-products of an entire product provides an AI-assisted environment supported by edge devices. The purpose of prototyping manufacturing-related products is to verify and determine the potential hazards or stability features of manufacturing products in a specified environment.

Engineers could experiment with their new designs or prototyping procedures using virtual reality-based solutions. In fact, modifying a window location in a house could have several side effects, i.e., the sunlight might be poor, visibility to specific spots in the environment may be reduced, and sound levels may be detrimentally affected. With the help of edge-assisted virtual reality solutions, the simulation environments could alter the positions of windows, doors, or other lighting conditions in house designs.

11.4 Edge-AI for agriculture

Edge-AI has been a beneficial supportive mechanism in agriculture. Broadly, agriculture-related applications are divided into two broad categories: (a) vegetation-related agriculture and (b) animal-related farming. Vegetation-related agriculture is a category that deals with the application of AI for monitoring crops, soil, plants, and associated accessories, whereas animal-related farming ensures the better living conditions of animals and monitoring them. Figure 11.4 classifies agricultural applications into two broad categories and illustrates the subtopics that will be covered in this section.

11.4.1 Vegetation-related agriculture

Agriculture that attempts to increase crop protection and plant growth falls under vegetation-related agriculture. The reason for involving edge-AI solutions in agriculture started with the need for innovations in the domain. The authors of [16] proposed an agricultural management system that collects information and predicts agricultural benefits based on LoRa technologies: an innovation. The root cause of the need for innovation in major agricultural-related applications is the demand for sustainable food crops in the future.

Edge-AI is utilized in vegetation-related agriculture to enable precision farming and increase the associated yields. There are several reasons that contribute to the necessity of having edge AI, as discussed below:

  1. Increase crop yield: AI-based solutions are applied in agriculture to increase crop yields. To do so, it is required to collect appropriate information about growing environments, weather, crop status, etc. Using this information, it is possible to predict productive environments and buyers.
  2. Minimize costs: An AI-powered agricultural setup can carefully select pesticides and optimize the requirement of fertilizers, considering the expenditures. For instance, the revenue of apples or mangoes could be increased by accurately detecting the ripeness of the fruit on a daily basis using AI-assisted drones. Similarly, coconuts are harvested using specialized AI-powered drones without manual intervention, and paddy fields are organized to report the daily status of weeds and insects.
  3. Automated irrigation: It is not necessary to pump water to a large hectare of land if weather prediction forecasts the possibility of rainfall in a region. An AI-assisted system can automatically drive the pumping system on land so that water is not wasted due to excessive water or near-future rainfalls. Also, the AI-powered solutions could determine the landscape of vegetation lands to detect the required amount of water for irrigation.
  4. Crop, soil, and water monitoring: Most of the agricultural solutions combined with AI are supported by appropriate monitoring systems. It is important to monitor the health status of crops, soil, and water in a timely way. In some cases, the water quality is not suitable for vegetation. For instance, laying crops in a high-pH region will not yield as expected. There are several remedies to counteract such variations in the pH value of water. An AI-assisted solution could prevent crops and actuate methods to increase the productivity of agriculture.
  5. Disease detection: Buds and smaller plants that are utilized as green vegetables are prone to diseases. Almost all trees require special attention on a daily or weekly basis to protect them from serious diseases or insects. In fact, it becomes difficult for owners or farmers to keep track of these different categories of diseases. For instance, coconut trees encounter several types of disease in the southern part of India. They are dry leaves, yellow leaves, insect bites, and caterpillar bites. These diseases could be automatically detected and forecast so that appropriate treatments are possible.
  6. Sustainable harvesting: Sustainable harvesting is a management process in which agriculture is handled in a natural way. For instance, utilizing rainwater, wind energy or solar-powered pumping stations, natural manure utilization, etc. In [1], the authors have studied the wider components, such as drones, for enabling sustainable harvesting techniques in agriculture.

11.4.2 Animal-related farming

Agriculture that improves the living conditions of animals, pets, and wildlife is categorized as animal-related farming. Edge-AI techniques could be applied in this domain in multiple ways. The most commonly utilized applications are described below.

11.4.2.1 Animal protection

Edge-AI techniques remain pivotal in protecting animals from danger. Wearable devices could be mounted on animals to constantly monitor their activities and detect health-related issues. Accordingly, veterinary doctors could be deployed to targeted locations to provide medicines and vitamins. Similarly, animals could be automatically warned or prevented from attacks using edge-enabled IoT solutions. For instance, elephants could be chased away from attacking other animals by automatically producing a buzz of bee swarms.

11.4.2.2 Animal emotion detection

Similar to human emotions, animals do express emotions in various ways. Identifying emotions such as happiness, sadness, anger, etc, could maintain animal welfare and keep them mentally healthy in the wild and in homes. It is crucial to detect the emotions of domestic animals as they live alongside humans.

Recently, researchers have studied the emotions of elephants. There have been efforts in the past to study the emotions of other animals, such as cats, dogs, pigs, and others. Emotion detection in animals is a subfield of affective computing in AI. The main idea behind identifying emotions is to study the state of animals using sophisticated deep learning techniques such as CNNs. In fact, several subparameters, such as heart rate, brain activity, facial expressions, and body gestures, can provide valuable information about the mental state of animals.

There are a few deep learning algorithms that can detect the emotions of animals. For instance, algorithms such as CNNs, ResNet, lightweight CNNs, and YOLOs can predict the emotions of animals based on pre-trained or custom-trained models. The algorithms have increased prediction accuracy if multi-modal information is fed into them. Notably, detecting the emotions of elephants using facial expressions and trumpeting noises can easily increase the prediction accuracy while detecting the emotions.

Animals exhibit emotions such as happiness, sadness, anger, fear, etc, [7, 8]. The detection of animal emotions includes psychology, biology, and veterinary medicine. Traditionally, emotions were manually detected by trained professionals or animal owners [11]. By suggesting remedial actions based on their emotions, the animals can live peaceful and happy lives in the wild or in homes. Also, the medication of animals could be monitored by assessing their emotions while treating them.

Detecting the emotions of animals is broadly classified based on the detection techniques as follows:

  • Invasive method: Invasive methods rely primarily on wearable sensors with normal biometric data and physiological indicators that are collected in real-time. This approach deals with individual animals. The major disadvantages of this approach includes handling a large volume of sensor data relating to emotions and mapping it with the characteristics of animals in real-time. Also, it is a challenging task to detect the emotions of a group of animals, as wearable sensors are often mounted on individual animals that are mobile in nature.
  • Non-invasive method: In contrast, non-invasive methods are based on collecting sensor data from facial expressions, animal behaviors, and body gestures. Among them, the facial expressions of animals are broadly utilized to study the emotions of animals. In the non-invasive method, sensors are mounted on nearby walls, trees, or room shelves to capture the gestures of animals. As the sensors are immobile, they can provide stable data for detecting emotions. The most commonly utilized emotions in animals include happiness, sadness, fear, disgust, neutral, etc. As mentioned earlier, the accuracy of predictions increases by adding multi-modal input data such as images, texts, voice, and video frames.
11.4.2.3 Wildlife conservation

Edge-AI systems can contribute to conserving wildlife species. In general, there are several programs and organizations involved in protecting forests or wild animals. For instance, tourist centers have adopted policies to provide a peaceful environment for wild animals, avoiding disturbing them throughout the day. There have been programs to organize sponsorship activities while animals are in distress. Also, organizations have become stricter in preventing forests becoming polluted by plastic waste from humans, which can detrimentally affect specific creatures.

A robust surveillance system could be adopted in forests to protect animals with the utilization of edge AI solutions. Animals could be rehabilitated in newer locations or forests if they are diligently monitored in real-time. In some situations, animals have to be relocated due to the scarcity of water or food materials, or they have to be provided with appropriate food by notifying the relevant forest officials. All these activities increase wildlife conservation with the assistance of edge-AI systems.

At times, especially during natural disasters such as floods or fire incidents, the animals living in forests have to be protected by assessing the remote locations and ensuring the requirement of assistive materials. Similarly, forest officials have to be protected from the wild animals that they are protecting. Hence, edge-enabled systems could become a solution to preserve wild animals and balance their livelihoods in contradictory environments.

11.4.2.4 Human–animal conflict resolution

Human–animal conflicts are not new issue. They have been in existence for centuries. However, the traditional approach of resolving such conflicts by killing animals has reduced the animal populations, especially a few endangered ones. This is because humans encroach on animals’ living spaces by increasing human populations.

Human–animal conflicts are very prominent in tropical countries such as India, Kenya, Costa Rica, etc. For instance, elephants attack humans at night. This is because people are gradually encroaching on forest locations to plant vegetation. Similarly, there are lion attacks in Kenya as they are often visited by wildlife enthusiasts, and there have been several news reports highlighting the attacks of bears on humans or police guards in Canada. Many of these incidents could be prevented by a specialized solution that could automatically notify humans about the dangers.

Traditionally, there have been approaches to providing electric fencing to prevent elephants from intruding on the farms of villagers. However, such fences have become a burden to several other endangered species living in the same region. Solutions such as edge-AI mechanisms that could detect the arrival of elephants and enable electric fences could be a solution to the problem.

11.4.2.5 Human–animal interaction

In practice, humans can communicate with each other using conversations that are learned in primary or secondary schools. However, animals do not have such structured learning systems. Obviously, there is a need for smooth interactions between humans and animals for several reasons, as listed below:

  1. Work Sharing: Many animals work alongside humans. For instance, dogs protect humans and hunt other animals; elephants carry logs based on the instructions provided by mahouts; donkeys are utilized to carry heavy loads, etc.
  2. Emotion Sharing: Animals are a stress-relievers for some humans. Many people spend a considerable amount of time interacting with their pets, such as cats, dogs, parrots, etc. The reactions and emotional intelligence shared between them have several advantages.
  3. Therapies: Animals are used to solve some medical issues that afflict humans. For instance, animals such as cats, dogs, horses, and so forth can provide therapy to humans when they are facing stress. A few animals play and birds sing to improve the mood and well-being of humans.

Edge-AI systems could enrich the interactions and relationships between humans and animals. It could also prevent conflicts. For instance, if elephants were hungry, they could cause physical damage to humans. Such potential problems could be identified, analyzed, and notified using edge-AI-based novel solutions [6].

11.4.2.6 Animal breed detection

Animal breed detection is a prominent example that utilizes edge-AI systems. It remains tougher for humans to exactly determine breeds and their characteristics. With the application of edge-AI-assisted technologies, videos could capture information and engineer features from the dataset to classify breeds with ease. For instance, the authors of [10] designed a methodology using edge AI to classify breeds among cows. The approach could be helpful to veterinary doctors or concerned authorities.

11.4.3 Use-case: elephant emotion detection framework using deep learning

Recently, we have designed a framework that predicts elephants’ emotions in real-time. The framework was integrated into a mobile edge device, e.g., a smartphone, to help people who are not animal experts gain access to elephants. The idea of performing elephant emotion detection is to increase the working capability of elephants by understanding their moods. The framework utilized labeled datasets of elephant emotions. Later, object detection and emotion detection was performed.

11.4.3.1 Traditional approach

Traditionally, object detection tasks consist of three subtasks:

  1. Region selection: In the past, regions from parsed video frames were utilized to detect appropriate regions where elephants are located. It resembles template matching and sliding windows with different aspect ratios and sizes for selecting those regions. In fact, this process required a large computational workforce and time.
  2. Feature extraction: Often, researchers used histograms or Haar classifiers to extract features from images. It was also practiced in the past to avoid potential issues such as occlusion, illumination, background noises, etc while extracting features from images.
  3. Classification: Traditionally, SVM or similar types of classifiers have been utilized to classify objects from images. Although several research works interested the market, the traditional classifiers kept simple processing steps for several applications and researchers.
11.4.3.2 Deep learning approach

The application of deep learning models for object detection and tracking has marked a unique scalable factor in innovations. It did not restrict the training to certain pre-defined manual features. In contrast, deep learning had the most robust representation in automating the feature engineering processes of data. The deep learning approach increased its cover by developing several learning algorithms that are categorized into two-stage detection and one-stage detection stages. Algorithms such as CNNs correspond to two-stage detections, and YOLO corresponds to one-stage detectors.

Unlike two-stage object detectors, the skipping of the region proposal process results in a shortening of inference time, which is valuable when performing real-time prediction. The relatively satisfied accuracy and low latency are reasons that YOLO is suitable for development as a mobile application. YOLOv5 inherited the strength of YOLO and made improvements with precision and the ability to detect multi-scale objects. A detailed description of different deep learning algorithms is discussed in [2].

11.4.3.3 Edge-AI lightweight approach

Edge-level intelligence in detecting the emotions of elephants is carried out using IoT and similar high-end technologies. The IoT technology combined with deep learning grows rapidly to automate predictions. It takes advantage of bringing deep learning applications closer to consumers. This real-time prediction is likely to be realized as a reduction in latency caused by data transmission between the edge and cloud.

In the elephant detection framework, containers are utilized to provide a lightweight solution. Containers share the host operating system kernel and only include the libraries and dependencies needed to run the application. This results in a smaller footprint and faster startup time. As an OS-virtualized software platform, a self-contained container resolves the dependency issue with the respective OS. Thus, it is easy to create, deploy, and run applications using tools such as Docker Compose. Docker avoids performance and cost overhead and ultimately offers better performance. It is able to manage the virtualization process and can, therefore, be used to distribute, deploy, and manage cloud and edge applications assigned to the clusters.

11.4.3.4 Elephant emotion feature detection

The emotion detection approach consisted of two pre-trained YOLOv5 models: (a) The first was the YOLOv5 model trained on the COCO dataset, which is used to detect the elephant. (b) The second was the YOLOv5 model that was trained with an elephant dataset with seven emotional features labeled: ‘happy eye’, ‘happy nose’, ‘happy mouth’, ‘sad eye’, ‘sad nose’, ‘angry eye’, and ‘angry ear’.

Once the model was created, inferring the emotions of elephants was detected by following the steps: (i) parsing video inputs, (ii) chopping them into frames, (iii) detecting elephants from frames, (iv) identifying bounding boxes and their positions, (v) classifying emotions with scores, and (vi)listing the scores and concluding the emotions. The entire framework involves mobile phones or edge-enabled devices to perform the intelligence on edge-level nodes.

11.4.3.5 Metrics utilized

To evaluate the algorithms associated with elephant emotion detection, we utilized the following metrics: (i) precision (equation (11.1)), (ii) recall (equation (11.2)), and mean average precision (mAP) (equation (11.4)).


$${precision} = \frac{\mathit{TF}}{\mathit{TP} + \mathit{FP}}$$
(11.1)


$${recall} = \frac{\mathit{TP}}{\mathit{TP} + \mathit{FN}}$$
(11.2)


$${accuracy} = \frac{\mathit{TP} + \mathit{TN}}{\mathit{TP} + \mathit{FN} + \mathit{FP} + \mathit{TN}}$$
(11.3)


$${mAP} = \frac{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{N}\mathit{AP}_{i}}{N}$$
(11.4)

where true positive (TP) means the cases in which the prediction was yes and the actual output was also yes; true negative (TN) means the cases in which the prediction was no and the actual output was also no; false positive (FP) means the cases in which the prediction was yes and the actual output was also no; false negative (FN) means the cases in which the prediction with NP and the actual output were also yes; AP means average precision; N means number of classes; TP means number of true positives; FP means number of false positives; FN means number of false negatives.

11.4.3.6 YOLO-based implementation details

To implement and capture elephant emotions, cameras were utilized in real-time. Additionally, as elephants are rarely found in many geographical locations, the videos available on YouTube links were considered for predicting emotions. In the work, a Pytorch framework was utilized to develop deep learning algorithms. The captured videos, either real-time screening videos or offline videos, were parsed and sniffed using OpenCVPython packages. The entire framework was integrated into a mobile phone, a type of edge device.

In the work, YOLOv5 was utilized as a single-shot detector to study the emotions of elephants. Initially, it is required to create a dataset that captures the emotions of elephants. The datasets are prepared such that the emotions are annotated using labels. In the work described above, we utilized labelImg software to create labels. Using the software, we could create bounding boxes to label them as distinguished classes. Here, to detect the emotions of elephants, the faces of elephants were labeled as happy, sad, fearful, and angry.

As we are familiar with the deep learning basics, it is a known fact that a training phase is initially required using algorithms to study the emotions of elephants. To do so, Python packages are imported into the implementation.

import torchimport torch.distributed as dist
import torch.nn as nn

Similarly, the YOLO model was imported to train the elephant’s emotions, as follows: from models.yolo import Model

from models.yolo import Model

After setting the required configurational parameters and paths where the trained model has to be saved, the elephant emotions are trained for a specified number of epochs, as follows:

for epoch in range(start_epoch, epochs):
callbacks.run(’on_train_epoch_start’)
model.train()

The implementation was parallelized using GPU-assisted CUDA programming, and the training models were saved at any point in time with specified states in checkpoints so that they could be restarted from any point in the training stage. In a Python-based implementation, the checkpoints and YOLO models are saved as follows:

torch.save(checkpoint, state)

Once the training is complete, the trained model is stored in a folder so that it can be utilized for further testing or validation purposes. The training and testing procedures are scripted such that commands are sufficient to train and test datasets. For instance, the training and testing features are activated using the following commands:

Training: python train.py --data data/elephant.yaml
--cfg models/yolov5s.yaml --batch-size 64
--name Model --epochs 2000
Testing: python detect.py --source Elephant --weights Model.pt

It can be observed that the training activity is carried out for elephant images that are labeled datasets. The training phase required the name of the model and the number of iterations for developing the model.

Later, the model was converted to a lightweight TFLite model so that it could be ported to small edge-enabled devices for further inferences. In the testing phase, the inferences are carried out in real-time by capturing the emotions of elephants using mobile devices.

11.5 Edge-AI for forensics

Forensics is a science that investigates crime situations using technologies and mechanisms. Traditionally, manual procedures have been adopted to detect crime-related investigations using forensic scientists. However, with the advent of AI and edge intelligence, forensics became a solid manifestation to identify criminals or crime scenarios [12]. The technologies assisted forensic scientists to detect situations with ease.

11.5.1 Forensic applications

Although forensics is important, it is better to assess possible situations in which artificial intelligence can play a vital role. Toward this end, a few lists of forensic applications are analyzed as follows:

  1. Site analysis: Detecting crime sites and investigating the probability of crime situations can be carried out with the assistance of modern IoT-enabled technologies. Evidence such as body tissues, hair, blood, etc, that relates to biological factors can be tracked using deep learning-assisted solutions. Similarly, evidence relating to tire pressure or the footprints of criminals could not be easily tracked by human eyes if potential variations were performed. It is crucial to detect the fingerprints and blood stains of criminals. IoT-enabled solutions or virtual reality-based metaverse solutions could be developed to accurately detect with explainability using edge-assisted AI solutions.
  2. Digital forensics: Digital forensics is a subfield of science that requires intelligence to learn from crime-related data. Additionally, it has to deal with the approach to storing and preserving data. In fact, data could be in several formats, such as texts, voice, video, or images. The application of sensor-enabled technologies at edge-level nodes enables faster investigation of cybercrimes. To preserve data and findings in forensics, blockchain-assisted AI technologies are utilized in digital forensics.
  3. Emotion/lie detection: Facial expressions could be tracked and replayed with sufficient evidence while applying edge-AI solutions. In general, the human brain has two main parts: the left hemisphere and the right hemisphere. The left hemisphere is used for analytics, and the right hemisphere is responsible for creativity. A person expressing the truth of a crime scenario turns their eyes to the left to collect information from the left hemisphere of the brain, whereas a person attempting to lie due to criminal offenses will more often move their eyes in the right direction. This means that the person wishes to create a new story as part of the creative situation. The lie might not be correctly detected by police investigators if they are not skilled. However, edge-AI solutions can easily capture such factors with ease and with explainable evidence.
  4. Handwriting/script analysis: Suicide often has specific evidence from the victims in terms of notes. Suicidal notes or scripts mentioning the cause of death by a person before the death are considered crucial evidence for forensic scientists. However, the task becomes extremely arduous for them to correctly analyze the writings owing to various factors; for instance, a person in fear or stress may not write properly. Hence, edge-AI-assisted algorithms are required to correctly analyze the handwritten scripts of suicidal notes or death notes.

11.5.2 Edge-AI requirements

Although there are several edge-AI advantages, the platform that supports forensics should be designed to protect data or evidence from potential defaulters. The learning models or prediction engines that handle data should not transfer the data to third-party providers, including cloud servers. Hence, edge-AI solutions are convincingly accepted by a wide sector of researchers and practitioners.

The edge devices that are involved in analysing the evidence from crime sites have to be well protected from security attacks or malware conditions. If not, there are chances of levying wrong decisions that could lead to incorrect conviction and imprisonment. It not only affects the psychological improvements of individuals but also the families belonging to victims.

Typically, edge-AI solutions can provide wrong inferences or evidence. The explainability of reaching such decisions could have flaws or inaccuracies. Obviously, the outcome could lead to failure. In order to prevent future implications, all evidence and inferences need to be well documented and preserved in specialized data storage units with robust technologies such as blockchains. The blockchain technology has an approach to preventing data tampering situations due to the offer of providing a trustless environment using a network of computing nodes.

The edge-AI systems have to integrate well with the traditional forensic mechanisms. For instance, the input from forensic scientists has to be integrated into existing training models. Also, models have to fit within the memory size of utilities or appliances that are utilized by forensic scientists. The edge-AI systems that are designed for forensics will have to support as many communication protocols as possible to deliver a sufficient quantity of services.

The services involving cloud providers have to be supportive of delivering appropriate information to scientists or users. There have been efforts in the past to study cloud-specific forensic information. Researchers have developed tools and libraries that could provide the necessary information from disks of virtual information to analyze crime sites or data. Similarly, there are requirements for edge-AI systems to capture cloud-specific data that is deleted or moved to other repositories. Such data could be a valuable source for providing evidence.

11.5.3 Edge-AI forensics: stages

Applying edge AI to forensics is carried out in various stages, especially when data are involved to process and collect evidence from crime sites. Typically, a more generic approach to performing forensics using edge-AI systems is listed as follows:

  • Resource identification: The first stage to performing forensics using edge-AI-supported platforms is to define the amount of resources that need to be involved. A few resources may be required to assess cloud-specific devices, some other resources may be utilized to assess edge-specific networking devices, some sensor-attached resources could be engaged in collecting data for the analysis, etc. Hence, it is important to understand the most possible resources that might be required in detecting crime sites.
  • Data collection: The next step is to collect the required data to analyze evidence using sensors or similar technologies nearer to the crime site. The sensors are connected to edge-enabled devices for faster processing of data and filtration of irrelevant data. Additionally, data that is associated with cloud services or third-party providers needs to be collected with sufficient indexes and storage manifestations for future analysis.
  • Data storage: After establishing sufficient evidence, the relevant data and details have to be well documented and stored in reusable storage units. The documentation, mostly, has to be written in legal terms and court-related law-firm terms so that lawyers and prosecutors will be in a position to express the edge-AI-supported evidence appropriately. Special attention has to be given to instantiating high security and replications for the data. Also, the tenure of this data storage has to be longer than 15 to 30 years owing to the diversion in cases even after the judgment of the courts.
  • Evidence delivery: The last stage of the edge-AI-supported forensic system is to deliver the findings in a legal format. Presenting the evidence has to be user-friendly for practitioners and forensic scientists. If possible, the delivery of evidence has to be represented at various levels, marking the complexity involved in the explainability of crime incidences. For instance, the public does not want to know the exact reason for specific evidence of crimes, whereas scientists will require detailed information about the reasons for such evidence and the countermeasures to prevent them in the future. The delivery of such evidence should spark future innovations that could be applied in the system as part of the learning experience of handling crime.

11.6 Edge-AI for mobility/logistics

AI-assisted solutions have been beneficial in the transportation sector while performing mobility or logistics in busy cities. For instance, traffic management, smart vehicular communications, autonomous vehicles, logistics planning, shared mobility, distributed product launches, etc, have been enriched with intelligence or learning assistance in modern products or solutions [9].

The importance of edge-AI solutions in the transportation sector is to deliver agile reasoning and innovative productization. This section details the application of edge AI in four broad applications in the transportation sector.

11.6.1 Smart mobility

Smart mobility has remained an important ingredient in smart cities. India has aimed to strengthen smart cities in 100 different regions. The major difference between a normal city and a smart city is the provision of vast support features that assist city residents. Smart mobility in smart cities has different application dimensions:

  1. Traffic flow management: Edge AI-supported applications can automatically drive traffic management in busy streets and city centers. The sensors need to be mounted on traffic corners, and edge device-supported intelligent algorithms can perform intelligence while controlling the traffic of vehicles. Intelligence can automate the traffic flow even when emergency vehicles need to travel during busy hours on the streets.
  2. Parking indicator: In many cities, there are several parking areas that are not seen by drivers while driving their vehicles. The edge-AI-supported parking lot identification system can detect empty lots for parking vehicles, considering the time to reach the parking destination and city preferences. This not only favors drivers but also the cities, preventing a large number of vehicles roaming around the busy streets searching for parking lots.
  3. Noise control: Smart mobility can ensure effective communication between vehicles using robust communication mediums. Recently, the enhanced utilization of 5G networks paved the way for vehicles to communicate with each other in the blind spots of cities or streets. This notion of vehicle-to-vehicle communications automatically controls the speed of vehicles and prevents potential accidents without the presence of edge-AI-based solutions. The practice of pressing horns all the way down to the street can be minimized to a negligible level if edge-AI-assisted solutions are regularly provided in all parts of the cities or countries.

11.6.2 Shared mobility intelligence

COVID-19 provided many innovations in the transportation sector, as COVID-19 patients were isolated from public transport. In fact, every country promotes shared mobility, where multiple passengers belonging to various communities and regions travel in a single vehicle such as a bus, taxi, train, or airplane. The countries gain economic improvements by motivating their citizens to utilize public transport with limited convenience compared to personal cars.

There were many challenges in the traditional shared mobility principles: (i) it was not possible to keep track of isolating patients in shared vehicles, (ii) collaboration between different departments, such as the justice department, police department, social security services, hospitals, etc, was not inherently practiced due to the non-availability of technologies, (iii) communication between vehicles and processing capabilities of devices were not sufficient to realistically handle situations, (iv) inefficient utilization of shared vehicles, etc.

The shared mobility assisted by edge AI and blockchains has recently marked a traction in the transportation sector. For instance, recently, a blockchain-enabled shared mobility (BESM) architecture was proposed to enhance the features of the shared mobility concept [4]. The synopsis of the architecture is to suggest that residents travel in a shareable vehicle, considering the air pollution status and COVID-19 status of regions. The idea behind performing such seat allocation is to increase the utility of shareable vehicles with more intelligence, taking into consideration the health status of humans.

To do so, an edge-AI system should capture the details of available shareable vehicles and the demand to utilize such vehicles at a particular time in a city or street. Additionally, a blockchain-based ledger needs to be started with a genesis block so that the transactions of seat allocation are recorded in the blockchain ledgers. To record the information in the ledger, the latitude and longitude of the traveler’s location, travel destination location, health information, current air quality information of travel destinations, etc, are collected and processed in the blockchain. Obviously, the information will be iteratively shared among all travel spots to regionally calculate the number of available seats in the shareable vehicle. Thus, several parameters with connected participants, including transport authorities and hospitals, are involved in deciding the seat allocation of a shareable vehicle to travelers. More detailed information about the architecture that can include edge-AI solutions or algorithms for allocating seats in a shareable vehicle is provided in [4].

11.6.3 Logistics planning

Automating services in smart cities is one of the primary tasks for city authorities. Planning logistics and automatically delivering services are part of the automation processes of smart cities. Edge-level intelligence has played a crucial role in performing automation processes by monitoring and analyzing data. One of the major objectives is to improve resource utilization and optimize logistics-related travel in cities.

Manhole detection and logistics planning are two of the applications under logistics planning. In fact, manholes are subject to failure, especially during the rainy season. There is a high chance that the level of manholes will increase, leading to drainage overflow. Or, manual monitoring and logistics planning to pick up full pipelines is a difficult task. Hence, in the past, work was undertaken to study the application of optimization and intelligence while planning the logistics for repairing manholes.

The manhole detection and logistic planning system consisted of sensors that monitored the depth of manholes. For this, ultrasonic sensors that detect the distance between obstacles are mounted below the manhole-closing lid. The sensor data is collected and processed using edge devices. The devices automatically parse data and preprocess it so that only relevant data is submitted to cloud or hierarchical computing nodes.

In the system, there were three services that benefited from the assistance of cloud-based resources. The services are listed as follows:

  • Manhole level indicator service: this service collects manhole levels from edge nodes that are connected to level-indicating sensors.
  • Logistics planner service: the purpose of this service is to collect transport details and the available resources, such as transportation vehicles, manpower (if any), etc.
  • Scheduler service: this service has codes that can schedule resources based on smart city information and manhole levels using scheduling algorithms. In the work, the threshold acceptance algorithm was utilized to plan the schedule to perform logistics in cities.

11.6.4 Product placement

Smart mobility is not just for vehicles or associated services. It can also play a pivotal role in indoor transportation. For instance, shopping malls are increasing day by day in cities, and small shops are being put out of business by these shopping giants. People prefer to purchase from such malls as they get a reduced price for products, and they are entertained an entire day while visiting such malls.

Transportation and logistic planning in huge malls within indoor environments could be a challenging aspect as several constraints are involved in the processes. Notably, the shops are interested in gaining business and attracting customers throughout the period of operations. For instance, placing products in the empty slots of showcases in malls is a mandatory step to attract customers and increase the revenue of businesses. This process requires a strong bond between edge-AI and mall-equipped devices.

In the past, an in-store product placement approach was developed using LiDAR sensors [5]. The idea behind this approach was to quickly identify the empty slots in showcases in supermarkets and replace them with customer preferences. In fact, the idea fits very well in huge shopping malls where shop employees cannot keep track of empty products or the preferences of customers. Also, the approach could be beneficial when the number of customers increases, especially during Christmas or Diwali holidays.

11.7 Conclusion

There are tens of thousands of edge-AI applications on the market. They are developed in various domains such as healthcare, industry, agriculture, transportation, digital forensics, etc. This chapter describes the potential edge-AI applications that are being experimented with by researchers or practitioners. The chapter showcases an approach involving edge-based mobile phones to detect the emotions of elephants. Additionally, a list of novel ideas has been shown in this chapter so that readers can explore the application of such ideas using frameworks or orchestration platforms discussed in the previous chapters of this book.
